\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\dsser{1}&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\left\llbracket b_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{1}\\
\dsser{0}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\lnot b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\dsser{1}&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{0}\\
\dsser{0}&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{1}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\llbracket t\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\dsser{1}&\text{if }\sigma\in t\\
\dsser{0}&\text{if }\sigma\not\in t\end{cases}

Appendix B
Domain Properties

Here, we prove a few preliminary results on our semantic domain
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
. The first of these describes how the Kleisli extension
(-)^{\dagger}:(X\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(Y))\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(Y)
interacts with
+
, scalar multiplication
\cdot
, and divergence:

Lemma B.1.

For any
f,g\in(X\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(Y))
,
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(X)
, and
u\in U
:

(1)

f^{\dagger}(m+m^{\prime})=f^{\dagger}(m)+f^{\dagger}(m^{\prime})

(2)

If
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\Sigma
,
(f+g)^{\dagger}(m)=f^{\dagger}(m)+g^{\dagger}(m)

(3)

f^{\dagger}(u\cdot m)=u\cdot f^{\dagger}(m)

(4)

If
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\Sigma
,
f^{\dagger}(m\cdot u)=f^{\dagger}(m)\cdot u

(5)

f^{\dagger}(\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))=\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Proof.

(1), (3), and (4) are left to the reader. We show:

(2)

If
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\Sigma
, then
m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\dsser{0}
. We have:

\displaystyle(f+g)^{\dagger}(m)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot(f+g)(\sigma)\bigg)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot(f(\sigma)+g(\sigma))

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)\bigg)+\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot g(\sigma)\bigg)

\displaystyle=f^{\dagger}(m)+g^{\dagger}(m)

(5)

Note that
\mathsf{supp}(\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))=\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\}
, so
\mathsf{supp}(\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))\cap\Sigma=\emptyset
. Then:

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}(\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)\bigg)+\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

∎

Appendix C
Totality of Language Semantics

C.1.
\mathcal{D}
-Closure

To accommodate both a continuous semantics and a (countably) infinite state set, it was necessary to restrict our domain to a subset
\mathcal{D}
of weighting functions. In particular, we identified two possible classes of semantics with corresponding restrictions on the semiring of weights
\mathcal{A}=\langle X,+,\cdot,\dsser{0},\dsser{1}\rangle
used:

•

Conservative
:
\mathcal{A}
is partial and bounded. Then, we take
\mathcal{D}\triangleq\{m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)\mid|m|=\top\}
.

•

Indicative
:
\mathcal{A}
is bounded with strongly-infinite top element
\top
. For any other semiring, we can adjoin an element
w\not\in X
and extend
+
and
\cdot
such that
w
is a strongly-infinite upper bound:

–

w+x=x+w=w
for all
x\in X
;

–

w\cdot x=x\cdot w=w
for all
x\in X\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}
;

–

w\cdot\dsser{0}=\dsser{0}\cdot w=\dsser{0}
.

In this case, take
\mathcal{D}\triangleq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
.

In this section we demonstrate that the program semantics is closed in
\mathcal{D}
for each of the classes described above.

C.1.1.
Conservative

Lemma C.1.

For any collection
(x_{i}\in X)_{i\in I}
such that
\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}=\top
, if
(m_{i})_{i\in I}
is a family of weighting functions with
|m_{i}|=\top
for each
i\in I
, then
\left|\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}\cdot m_{i}\right|=\top
.

Proof.

It holds that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}\cdot m_{i}\right|=\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}\cdot|m_{i}|=\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}\cdot T=\left(\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}\right)\cdot\top=\top\cdot\top=\top

∎

Lemma C.2.

For any
m\in\mathcal{D}
and
f\in\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
,
\mathsf{bind}(m,f)\in\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\Sigma)\cup\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
. First, suppose that
m,f(\sigma)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\Sigma)
(i.e.
m
and
f(\sigma)
have finite support) for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma
. Recall:

\mathsf{bind}(m,f)=m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)

So,

\mathsf{supp}(\mathsf{bind}(m,f))=(\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\})\cup\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}\mathsf{supp}(f(\sigma))

Since
\mathsf{supp}(m)
is finite, and
\mathsf{supp}(f(\sigma))
is finite for each
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma
, the above expression consists of a finite union of finite sets, which is itself finite. Thus,
\mathsf{bind}(m,f)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\Sigma)\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
.

Next, we consider the case in which
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
or
f(\sigma)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
for some
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma
. We split our analysis across the two weighting schemes defined (and, accordingly, the two definitions for
\mathcal{D}
provided for each):

•

Conservative Weighting
:
\mathcal{D}=\{m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\mid|m|=\top\}
.

Since
m,f(\sigma)\in\mathcal{D}
for every
\sigma\in\Sigma
, it holds that

–

(m(\sigma)\in X)_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}
is a family of weights such that
\left|\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\right|=|m|=\top
;

–

|f(\sigma)|=\top
for each
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma
;

–

|\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}|=\top
.

Thus, by
lemma

C.1
,
|\mathsf{bind}(m,f)|=\top
.

•

Indicative Weighting
:
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
.

If
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
, then
m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\sup_{m^{\prime}\in E(m)}m^{\prime}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\top
, indicating the presence of nontermination. As a strong infinity,
\top
is absorbing and thus propagates through
\mathsf{bind}(m,f)
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{bind}(m,f)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\top\cdot\top\cdot\dsser{1}+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\top+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\top=\sup_{m^{\prime}\in E(\mathsf{bind}(m,f))}m^{\prime}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Similarly, if
f(\tau)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
for some
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{bind}(m,f)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\big(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\dsser{1}\big)+\big(m(\tau)\cdot f(\tau)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma/\{\tau\}}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\big(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\dsser{1}\big)+\big(m(\tau)\cdot\top\big)+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma/\{\tau\}}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Since
\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
, we know
m(\tau)\neq 0
, so
m(\tau)\cdot\top=\top
:

\displaystyle=\big(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\dsser{1}\big)+\top+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma/\{\tau\}}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\top=\sup_{m^{\prime}\in E(\mathsf{bind}(m,f))}m^{\prime}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Therefore,
\mathsf{bind}(m,f)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)=\mathcal{D}
.

∎

C.2.
Fixpoint Existence

Theorem C.3 (Scott-Continuity of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
).

Suppose
\mathcal{A}
is a partial semiring which is bounded, finitary, Scott-continuous, and lower Scott-continuous. Then,
+:\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
on weighting functions is Scott-continuous with respect to the
fusion order

\sqsubseteq
. That is, for any directed subset
D\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
,

\sup_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}+m_{2})=\sup D+m_{2}

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma
. Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{m_{1}\in D}((m_{1}+m_{2})(\sigma))

\displaystyle=\sup_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}(\sigma)+m_{2}(\sigma))

\displaystyle=\sup_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}(\sigma))+m_{2}(\sigma)

(Scott-continuity of
\mathcal{A}
)

\displaystyle=\big(\sup_{m_{1}\in D}m_{1}(\sigma)\big)+m_{2}(\sigma)

Dually,

\displaystyle\inf_{m_{1}\in D}((m_{1}+m_{2})({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

\displaystyle=\inf_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})+m_{2}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

\displaystyle=\inf_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})+m_{2}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

(Lower Scott-continuity of
\mathcal{A}
)

\displaystyle=\big(\inf_{m_{1}\in D}m_{1}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)+m_{2}({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Now, since fusion order extends
\leq
pointwise on
\Sigma
and
\geq
on
{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}
, we have that
\sup_{m_{1}\in D}(m_{1}+m_{2})=\sup D+m_{2}
.

∎

Lemma C.4 (Joint Continuity).

Suppose
X
is a dcpo. For any function
f:X\times X\rightarrow X
that is Scott-continuous in the variables separately,
f
must be continuous in the variables jointly as well. I.e. for directed subsets
D_{1},D_{2}\subseteq X
,

\sup_{x\in D_{1},y\in D_{2}}f(x,y)=f(\sup D_{1},\sup D_{2})

Proof.

Since
f
is continuous separately in the first and second variables:

\displaystyle f(\sup D_{1},\sup D_{2})=\sup_{x\in D_{1}}f(x,\sup D_{2})=\sup_{x\in D_{1}}\sup_{y\in D_{2}}f(x,y)=\sup_{x\in D_{1},y\in D_{2}}f(x,y)

∎

Lemma C.5.

Let
\langle X,+,\cdot,0,1\rangle
be a finitary (complete), continuous, partial semiring. For any family of Scott-continuous functions
(f_{i}:X\rightarrow X)_{i\in I}
and directed set
D\subseteq X
:

\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(x)=\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(\sup D)

Proof.

Since each
f_{i}
is Scott-continuous, we have
\{f_{i}(x)\mid x\in D\}
is a directed set. We now proceed by induction on
I
.

•

Base Case:
I=\{i_{1}\}
. Then, we only need to show
\sup_{x\in D}f_{i_{1}}(x)=f_{i_{1}}(\sup D)
, which follows from the Scott-continuity of
f_{i_{1}}
.

•

Successor Case: Suppose the claim holds for all set smaller than
I
, for
I
finite. We can partition
I
into disjoint non-empty parts
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, with
I=I_{1}\cup I_{2}
,
I_{1}\cap I_{2}=\emptyset
, and
I_{1},I_{2}\neq\emptyset
. So,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in D}\bigg(\sum_{i\in I_{1}}f_{i}(x)+\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f_{i}(x)\bigg)

By the induction hypothesis, each of
\lambda x.\>\sum_{i\in I_{1}}f_{i}(x)
and
\lambda x.\>\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f_{i}(x)
are continuous, so
\{\sum_{i\in I_{1}}f_{i}(x)\mid x\in D\}
and
\{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f_{i}(x)\mid x\in D\}
are directed. We apply joint continuity of
+:X^{2}\rightarrow X
to get:

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in I_{1}}f_{i}(x)+\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f_{i}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{1}}f_{i}(\sup D)+\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f_{i}(\sup D)

(IH)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(\sup D)

•

Limit Case: Suppose the claim holds for all finite indexing sets. Since the semiring is finitary:

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in D}\bigg(\sup_{J\subseteq I,\text{ $J$ finite}}\sum_{i\in J}f_{i}(x)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sup_{J\subseteq I,\text{ $J$ finite}}\bigg(\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{i\in J}f_{i}(x)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sup_{J\subseteq I,\text{ $J$ finite}}\bigg(\sum_{i\in J}f_{i}(\sup D)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(\sup D)

∎

Corollary C.6.

For any
finite
family of Scott-continuous functions
\big(f_{i}:\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})\big)_{i\in I}
and directed set
D\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
:

\sup_{m\in D}\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(m)=\sum_{i\in I}f_{i}(\sup D)

Proof.

See the proof of
C.5
up to the successor case.
∎

Lemma C.7.

For any
\sigma\in\Sigma
, the function
g(f)=m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)(\tau)
is Scott-continuous in the semiring
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}g(f)

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in D}m(\sigma)\cdot f(\sigma)(\tau)

By Scott-continuity of the
\cdot
operator in the semiring:

\displaystyle=m(\sigma)\cdot\sup_{f\in D}f(\sigma)(\tau)

Since we are using the pointwise* ordering:

\displaystyle=m(\sigma)\cdot(\sup D)(\sigma)(\tau)=g(\sup D)

∎

Corollary C.8.

For
D\subseteq(\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}))
directed,

\sup_{f\in D}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot(\sup D)(\sigma)(\tau)=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot(\sup D)(\sigma)(\tau)

Proof.

By Lemma
C.5
.
∎

Lemma C.9.

If
m\in\mathcal{D}
and
f\in(\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{D})
,
\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)
is Scott-continuous with respect to its second argument
f
.

Proof.

Take
D\subseteq\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
directed. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma
and
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\in\mathcal{D}
. Recall that
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(\Sigma)=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\Sigma)\cup\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
, so we can split our analysis across two cases:

•

Suppose
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\Sigma)
. Then,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))
is finite, as is
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cap\Sigma
.

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in D}\bigg(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}+\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cap\Sigma)}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot f(\tau)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}+\sup_{f\in D}\bigg(\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cap\Sigma)}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot f(\tau)\bigg)

Since the above summation is finite (by assumption), we can apply (Corollary):

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}+\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap\Sigma}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot(\sup D)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)

•

Otherwise,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
. We know that for any program state
\tau^{\prime}\in\Sigma
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in D}\bigg(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap\Sigma}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot f(\tau)(\tau^{\prime})\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sup_{f\in D}\bigg(\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap\Sigma}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot f(\tau)(\tau^{\prime})\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\top\cdot\delta_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}(\tau^{\prime})+\sum_{\tau\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap\Sigma}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\cdot(\sup D)(\tau)(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)(\tau^{\prime})

In other words,
\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)\in E(\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D))
, so by the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\omega}(\Sigma)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\>

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\>\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Conversely,
\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)\in E(\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f))
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\>

\displaystyle\sqsupseteq\>\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Thus,
\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\mathsf{bind}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})
.

∎

Lemma C.10.

Let
\Phi_{\langle C,e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}(f)(\sigma)=\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\mathsf{bind}_{\mathsf{nt}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)+\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)
and suppose that it is a total function, then
\Phi_{{C}^{\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}}
is Scott continuous with respect to the pointwise order:
f_{1}\sqsubseteq f_{2}
iff
f_{1}(\sigma)\sqsubseteq f_{2}(\sigma)
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
.

Proof.

For all directed sets
D\subseteq(\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}(\Sigma\cup\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\})
and
\sigma\in\Sigma
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in D}\Phi_{{C}^{\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}}(f)(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in D}\big(\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\mathsf{bind}_{\mathsf{nt}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)+\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)\big)

By the continuity of
+
and
\cdot
in
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}
:

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\bigg(\sup_{f\in D}\mathsf{bind}_{\mathsf{nt}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),f)\bigg)+\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

By Lemma 6 (the previous Lemma):

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e_{1}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\mathsf{bind}_{\mathsf{nt}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma),\sup D)+\left\llbracket e_{2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{{C}^{\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}}(\sup D)(\sigma)

As this holds for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, we also have that

\sup_{f\in D}\Phi_{{C}^{\langle e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}}(f)=\Phi_{\langle C,e_{1},e_{2}\rangle}(\sup D)

∎

Appendix D
Subsumption of Program Logics

Here, we prove the results of
Section

4
on the subsumption of classical program logics. For this, recall that we limit ourselves to a nondeterministic interpretation of TOL, instantiated on the Boolean semiring.
First, we show that the encodings of modalities
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}
of Dynamic Logic in TOL are DeMorgan duals.

Lemma D.1 (Modal Duality).

{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P=\neg{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}\neg P\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P=\neg{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}\neg P

Proof.

\displaystyle\neg{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}\neg P

\displaystyle=\neg\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq(\neg P)_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})\setminus\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq(\neg P)_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\not\subseteq(\neg P)_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap P\neq\emptyset\}\quad={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P

\displaystyle\neg{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}\neg P

\displaystyle=\neg\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\neg P\neq\emptyset\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})\setminus\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\neg P\neq\emptyset\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\neg P=\emptyset\}

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\}\quad={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P

∎

Moreover, the alternative modalities
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}
exhibit the same duality. Proof of this is nearly identical to the above, and so is omitted.

Theorems
4.1
and
4.2
state that Hoare, Lisbon, Total Hoare, as well as angelic partial correctness triples can be encoded using the above modalities. These correspond to the logics in the front upper cube of
Figure

5
which are formulated in terms of weakest-precondition transformers.

Theorem
4.1
(Subsumption of Hoare Logic).

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\text{iff}\quad P\subseteq\mathsf{dwlp}(C,Q)
.

Proof.

\implies
: Suppose
\sigma\in P
. Then,
\eta(\sigma)\vDash P
. We assume
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, so

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\eta(\sigma))

\displaystyle\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q\>\>=\>\>\exists(u,v)\in U^{2}.\>\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil^{(u)}\oplus\Uparrow^{(v)}\>\>=\>\>\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq Q_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\}

That is,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}
. By definition
\sigma\in\mathsf{dwlp}(C,Q)
.

\impliedby
: We assume
\vDash P\subseteq\mathsf{dwlp}(C,Q)
. Suppose
m\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. So,
|m|=1
and
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq\mathsf{dwlp}(C,Q)
. Note that
{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\not\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
. Then, since
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m))

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\quad\subseteq\quad Q_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}

By definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}
,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q
.

∎

Theorem
4.1
(Subsumption of Lisbon Logic).

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\text{iff}\quad P\subseteq\mathsf{awp}(C,Q)
.

Proof.

\implies
: Suppose
\sigma\in P
. Then,
\eta(\sigma)\vDash P
. Since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\eta(\sigma))\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q\>\>=\>\>\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil^{(u)}\oplus\top\>\>=\>\>\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap Q\neq\emptyset\}

This means
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q\neq\emptyset
, so
\sigma\in\mathsf{awp}(C,Q)
by definition.

\impliedby
: Assume
P\subseteq\langle Q\rangle
. Suppose
m\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, i.e.
|m|=1
,
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq\mathsf{awp}(C,Q)
. Note that
{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\not\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
. So, for any
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q\neq\emptyset
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{supp}\left(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)\cap Q

\displaystyle=\left(\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\right)\cap Q=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\left(\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q\right)\neq\emptyset

By definition,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q
.

∎

Theorem
4.2
(Subsumption of Total Hoare Logic).

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\text{iff}\quad P\subseteq\mathsf{dwp}(C,Q)
.

Proof.

\implies
: Suppose
\sigma\in P
. Then,
\eta(\sigma)\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
,

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\eta(\sigma))\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q=\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil^{(u)}=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq Q\}

That is,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q
, so we have
\sigma\in\mathsf{dwp}(C,Q)
.

\impliedby
: Suppose
m\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, i.e.
|m|=1
and
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq\mathsf{dwp}(C,Q)
. Note that
{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\not\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
, and

\mathsf{supp}\left(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))

For each
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\subseteq Q
. This gives us that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q
, as desired.

∎

Theorem
4.2
(Subsumption of Angelic Partial Correctness).

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\text{iff}\quad P\subseteq\mathsf{awlp}(P,Q)

Proof.

\implies
: Suppose
\sigma\in P
. Then,
\eta(\sigma)\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
,

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\eta(\sigma))\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}Q

\displaystyle=\exists(u,v):U^{2}\setminus\{(\dsser{0},\dsser{0})\}.\>\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil^{(u)}\oplus\Uparrow^{(v)}\oplus\top

\displaystyle=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap Q_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}\neq\emptyset\}

That is,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\neq\emptyset
, so we have
\sigma\in\mathsf{awlp}(C,Q)
.

\impliedby
: Suppose
m\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, i.e.
|m|=1
and
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq P\subseteq\mathsf{awlp}(C,Q)
. By definition, for any
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
,
\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\neq\emptyset
. It holds then that

\mathsf{supp}\left(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)\cap Q_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}=\left(\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\right)\cap Q_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\left(\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))\cap Q_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\right)\neq\emptyset

This gives us
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}Q
, as desired.

∎

Appendix E
Soundness and Relative Completeness

Here, we prove the soundness and (relative) completeness of
TOL
. First, some preliminary definitions:

Definition E.1.

For any test
b\in\dsser{2}^{\Sigma}
and
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
, we define the
projection
of
m
onto
b
to be the following weighting function:

(b\>?\>m)(\sigma)\triangleq\begin{cases}m(\sigma)&\text{if }\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{1}\\
0&\text{if }\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{0}\text{ or }\sigma={\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

In our proofs, it will be useful to decompose a program configuration
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{\mathord{\circlearrowright}})
into two – one part which solely describes weights on program states
\Sigma
, and another which solely describes the degree of nontermination. We introduce the following notational devices:

Definition E.2.

Suppose
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
. We define
\textsf{term}\>?\>m\triangleq\mathsf{true}\>?\>m
and
\textsf{div}\>?\>m\triangleq m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})
to be the stateful and nonterminating components of
m
, respectively. These are weighting functions:

\displaystyle(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}m(\sigma)&\text{if }\sigma\in\Sigma\\
\dsser{0}&\text{if }\sigma=\>{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle(\textsf{div}\>?\>m)(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\dsser{0}&\text{if }\sigma\in\Sigma\\
m(\sigma)&\text{if }\sigma=\>{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

We give some properties for the projection operator:

Lemma E.3.

The projection operator
(b\>?\>-)
commutes with the following operations:

(i)

\sum_{i\in I}(b\>?\>m_{i})=b\>?\>\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}

(ii)

u\cdot(b\>?\>m)=b\>?\>u\cdot m
and
(b\>?\>m)\cdot u=b\>?\>m\cdot u

Moreoever, for any outcome assertion
\varphi
:

(ii)

if for all
m\in\varphi
,
m=b\>?\>m^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}
, then
\varphi\vDash b

(iv)

if for all
m\in\varphi
,
m=\textsf{div}\>?\>m^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}
, then
\varphi\vDash\mathsf{div}
.

Proof.

(i) and (ii): We show that
\left(u\cdot\sum_{i\in I}(b\>?\>m_{i})\cdot v\right)(\sigma)=\left(b\>?\>u\cdot\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}\cdot v\right)(\sigma)
. From the definition of
projection
, it is straightforward to see that these are equal to

\begin{cases}u\cdot\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}(\sigma)\cdot v&\text{if }\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{1}\\
\dsser{0}&\text{if }\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\dsser{0}\text{ or }\sigma={\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

(iii): Suppose that for all
m\in\varphi
,
m=b\>?\>m^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}
. Then, it should be clear that for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)=\mathsf{supp}(b\>?\>m^{\prime})
,
\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\textsf{Test}}(\sigma)=1
. By definition,
\varphi\vDash b
. (iv) holds by a similar argument.
∎

Corollary E.4.

\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(b\>?\>m)=b\>?\>\left(\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)

Proof.

\displaystyle b\>?\>\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad=b\>?\>\left[\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}b\>?\>\left[m(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\right]+b\>?\>m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}b\>?\>\left[m(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}b\>?\>\left[m(\sigma)\right]\cdot\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(b\>?\>m)\cap\Sigma}b\>?\>\left[m(\sigma)\right]\cdot\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)

Note that this is precisely
\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(b\>?\>m)
.
∎

We proceed by proving some results on the semantics of iteration. Recall that the semantics for a command
{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}
is given in terms of the characteristic function
\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}:(\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}))\rightarrow\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
:

\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}(f)(\sigma)=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot f^{\dagger}\big(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\big)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

The following Lemma states that applications of
\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}
starting on
\bot
are equivalent to
unrolling

{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}
a corresponding number of times.

Lemma E.5.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma
, and
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}
,

3
3

3

Our semantics must capture two computational effects – weighted execution and nontermination – each of which compose differently when programs are sequenced. As a result, the unrolling is expressed differently depending on whether we want the final weight on a program state in
\Sigma
or the final weight of the nontermination outcome. In the case of the former, we aggregate the weight collected for traces terminating within the first
n
iterations. In the latter, we push any weight of traces not yet terminated (including any divergent weight from potential inner loops) onto
{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}
.

\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+1}(\bot)(\sigma)(\tau)=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)&\text{if }\tau\in\Sigma\\
\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n+1}\right\rrbracket(\sigma))({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})&\text{if }\tau=\>{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

Note: For a command
C
, we define
C^{0}\triangleq\texttt{skip}
and
C^{n+1}\triangleq C^{n}\fatsemi C
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

•

n=0
. We have that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}(\bot)(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\bot^{\dagger}\big(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\big)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))+\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)

Now, if
\tau\in\Sigma
, then
\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)\right\rrbracket(\sigma))(\tau)=\dsser{0}
, since
\mathsf{supp}(\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))=\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\}
. On the other hand,
\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\dsser{0}
. This gives us the two cases, as desired.

•

Inductive step
. Suppose the claim holds for
n
. First,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+2}(\bot)(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\big(\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+1}(\bot)\big)^{\dagger}\big(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\big)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)+\eta(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau^{\prime})\cdot\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+1}(\bot)(\tau^{\prime})\bigg)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)

Now, for
\tau\in\Sigma
, the induction hypothesis gives us:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+2}(\bot)(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\left(\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)}}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau^{\prime})\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\tau^{\prime})(\tau)\right)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau^{\prime})\cdot\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\tau^{\prime})(\tau)\right)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left(\>\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))}\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau^{\prime})\cdot\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\tau^{\prime})(\tau)\right)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left(\>\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)\right)+\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)

Applying the other case of the induction hypothesis:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+2}(\bot)(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma))}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau^{\prime})\cdot\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n+1}\right\rrbracket(\tau^{\prime}))({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\bigg)+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n+2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma))({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})+\left\llbracket e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\dsser{0}

\displaystyle=\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n+2}\right\rrbracket(\sigma))({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

∎

We can now reformulate the semantics of
{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}
by unrolling the command iteration-by-iteration:

Lemma E.6 (Unrolling).

For all
\sigma\in\Sigma
and
\tau\in\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}
,

\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)=\begin{cases}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)&\text{if }\tau\in\Sigma\\
\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket(\sigma))(\tau)&\text{if }\tau=\>{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\end{cases}

Proof.

By the program semantics (
fig.

4
) and Kleene’s fixed point theorem,

\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)=\textsf{lfp}~f.\>\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}(f)(\sigma)=\sup_{n\in N}\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n}(\bot)(\sigma)

Now, suppose
\tau\in\Sigma
. Since
\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{0}(\bot)=\bot
, or the bottom of the order on
(\Sigma\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}}))
, we can rewrite the supremum as:

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n}(\bot)(\sigma)(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n+1}(\bot)(\sigma)(\tau)

By
lemma

E.5
:

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{k}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)

By the definition of infinite sums:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket(\sigma)(\tau)

Finally, we can obtain the remaining case using
E.5
:

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Phi_{\langle C,e,e^{\prime}\rangle}^{n}(\sigma)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n})\right\rrbracket)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)

\displaystyle=\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bot^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n})\right\rrbracket)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

∎

Theorem E.7 (Soundness).

\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\implies\quad\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

Proof.

The triple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
is proved using inference rules given in
fig.

4
and
fig.

3
. If the last step in this proof makes use of an axiom, then we are done since we took all axioms in
\Omega
to be semantically valid. Otherwise, we proceed by induction on the derivation of
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

•

False
. We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bot{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\bot
. But this is impossible, so the claim holds vacuously.

•

True
. We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\varphi
. It is trivial that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\top
, so we are done.

•

Div
. We want to demonstrate
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\>\Uparrow^{(u)}
. Then,
m=u\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})
by definition. We have

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}\big(u\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)+\big(u\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}\dsser{0}\cdot\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket(\sigma)+u\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=u\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

So,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\>\Uparrow^{(u)}
.

•

Scale
. By the induction hypothesis, we have
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u\odot\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u\odot\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash u\odot\varphi
. By definition,
m=u\cdot m^{\prime}
where
m^{\prime}\vDash\varphi
. Using (iii) of
B.1
, it follows that

\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(u\cdot m^{\prime})=u\cdot\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})

Since
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})\vDash\psi
, we can conclude that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash u\odot\psi
.

•

Disj
. By the induction hypothesis,
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
and
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. We want to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
. Without loss of generality, take
m\vDash\varphi_{1}
. The induction hypothesis gives us
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}
, which can be weakened to
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}
. The case for
m\vDash\varphi_{2}
is symmetric.

•

Conj
. We have
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
and
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
by the induction hypothesis and we want to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\land\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Supposing
m\vDash\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
, it holds that
m\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
m\vDash\varphi_{2}
. Then,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}
and
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{2}
. By definition,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}\land\psi_{2}
, as desired.

•

Choice
. By the induction hypothesis, we have that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\phi(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\phi^{\prime}(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
for all
t\in T
. We now show that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{t\in T}\phi(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{t\in T}\phi^{\prime}(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\bigoplus_{t\in T}\phi(t)
. By definition, this means
m=\sum_{t\in T}m_{t}
such that for all
t\in T
,
m_{t}\vDash\phi(t)
. Since
\sum
commutes with
(-)^{\dagger}
in the first argument by
B.1
,

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\left(\sum_{t\in T}m_{t}\right)=\sum_{t\in T}\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m_{t})

For each
m_{t}
,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m_{t})\vDash\phi^{\prime}(t)
, giving us
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\bigoplus_{t\in T}\phi^{\prime}(t)
.

•

Exists
. By the induction hypothesis, we are given
\forall t\in T.\>\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\phi(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\phi^{\prime}(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists t:T.\>\phi(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists t:T.\>\phi^{\prime}(t){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\exists t:T.\>\phi(t)
.
By definition, this means
m\in\bigcup_{t\in T}\phi(t)
, so there must be some
t\in T
for which
m\in\phi(t)
. Then, by the induction hypothesis,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\phi^{\prime}(t)
, we have that
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\exists t:T.\>\phi^{\prime}(t)
.

•

Consequence
. Given to us are
\vDash\varphi^{\prime}\implies\varphi
and
\vDash\psi\implies\psi^{\prime}
, and we have
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
by the induction hypothesis. We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\varphi^{\prime}
. Then,
m\vDash\varphi
and
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi
. Finally, this gives us
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi^{\prime}
as desired.

•

Skip
. We need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{skip}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose that
m\vDash\varphi
. We have that
\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=m
, so
\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\varphi
as desired.

•

Seq
. Given
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
and
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, we need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Suppose
m\vDash\varphi
, so by the induction hypothesis,
\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\vartheta
and
\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m))\vDash\psi
. Since
\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m))=(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\circ\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!)(m)=\left\llbracket C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
, we are done.

•

Plus
. Given
\varphi\vDash\dsser{1}
,
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, and
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, we need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. First, suppose
m\vDash\varphi
. Since
\varphi\vDash\dsser{1}
, it must be that
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\Sigma
. By
B.1
, we know that the
(-)^{\dagger}
operator commutes with
+
in its first argument:

\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)+\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

By the induction hypothesis,
\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}
and
\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{2}
, so we have
\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}
.

•

Assume
. Suppose
\varphi\vDash e=u
. Recall that this holds iff

\forall m\in\varphi.\>m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=0\text{ and }\forall\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma.\>\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)=u.

We need to show
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ u{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. Take
m\vDash\varphi
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~\right\rrbracket(\sigma)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot u\cdot\eta(\sigma)+0

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\cap\Sigma}m(\sigma)\cdot\eta(\sigma)\right)\cdot u

\displaystyle=m\cdot u

By definition,
m\cdot u\vDash\varphi\odot u
, as desired.

•

Iter
. We need to show that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}\oplus\zeta_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}\oplus\zeta_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, given the premises. Suppose
m\vDash\varphi_{0}\oplus\zeta_{0}
. By the induction hypothesis, we have that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

By mathematical induction on
n
, it is straightforward to show that

(2)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}

(3)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle\vDash\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}

for all
n
. Note that
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
since
\zeta_{n}\vDash\mathsf{div}
. In particular, we have that for any
m\vDash\zeta_{n}
,
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\}
, so
\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=m
.

Consider each of these results separately:

(1)

For all
n
,
\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
. Then, there exist
p_{n},q_{n}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
such that

\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=p_{n}+q_{n}

for
p_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
and
q_{n}\vDash\zeta_{n}
. Since
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}
, it must be that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}p_{n}\vDash\psi_{\infty}
.

(2)

For all
n
,
\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
. Since

(\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}
, we have

\left(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big|\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big|\cdot\top\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\vDash\zeta_{\infty}

We can now combine the two parts. By
E.6
, the unrolling of
\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
is

\displaystyle\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)|\cdot\top\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

Therefore,
\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\psi_{\infty}\oplus\zeta_{\infty}
.

∎

Paving a path to relative completeness, we first prove that triples of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
can be derived, where
\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)
is the
strongest postcondition
that makes
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
true. Defined otherwise:

Definition E.8 (Strongest Postcondition).

\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)
consists of all program configurations reachable from
\varphi
by executing command
C
:

\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)\triangleq\{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\}

Lemma E.9.

\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

Proof.

By induction on the structure of the program
C
.

•

C=\texttt{skip}
.
\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)=m
for all
m\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma_{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}})
, so
\mathsf{post}(\texttt{skip},\varphi)=\varphi
. The desired triple is derived by one application of the
Skip
rule:

\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Skip}

•

C=C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}
. First, observe that

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m))\mid m\in\varphi\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})\mid m^{\prime}\in\{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\}\}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi))

By the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

The derivation is completed with the
Seq
rule:

\frac{\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\varphi)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}

•

C=C_{1}+C_{2}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(C_{1}+C_{2},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

By
B.1
(i), this is equal to:

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m\big)+\left\llbracket C_{1}+C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

Observe that
\mathsf{supp}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\subseteq\Sigma
. Applying (ii) and (iv) of
B.1
:

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m\big)+\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m\big)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{m\in\varphi}\big\{\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(m^{\prime}\big)+\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(m^{\prime}\big)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m^{\prime}\in\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\big\}

\displaystyle=\exists m:\varphi.\>\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\oplus\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\>\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

We break the derivation into parts. First, note that
\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\vDash\textsf{term}
for any
m
. Then, we have the subderivation
(*)
:

\small\frac{\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{vdots=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m))\oplus\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Plus,sep=-7em}

The full derivation is completed as follows:

\small\frac{\forall m\in\varphi.\>\>\frac{\frac{(*)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Plus}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Div}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m))\oplus\mathsf{post}(C_{2},\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m))\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Choice, sep=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}+C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C_{1}+C_{2},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Exists}

•

C=\texttt{assume}~e
, where
e
must either be a test
b\in\textsf{Test}
or weight
u\in U
. First, we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~e,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m\big)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

Recall that, by
E.1
,

\textsf{term}\>?\>m=b\>?\>(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)+\neg b\>?\>(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)=b\>?\>m+\neg b\>?\>m

So
\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)=\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)+\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m)
. Then, we have that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~b,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\big\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~b\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(b\>?\>m+\neg b\>?\>m)+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\big\{b\>?\>m+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\exists m:\varphi.\>\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)+\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

It should be clear that
b\>?\>m\vDash b=\dsser{1}
and
\neg b\>?\>m\vDash b=\dsser{0}
. Then, we have
(*)
:

\small\frac{\frac{\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\vDash b=\dsser{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\odot\dsser{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Assume}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m)\vDash b=\dsser{0}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\odot\dsser{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Assume}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\oplus\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Choice}\\

The derivation is completed as follows:

\small\frac{\forall m\in\varphi.\>\>\frac{\frac{(*)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\oplus\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Choice}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Div,vdots=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\oplus\mathbf{1}(\neg b\>?\>m)\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(b\>?\>m)\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Choice,sep=-2em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~b,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Exists}

Suppose instead that
e
is a weight
u\in U
. Since
\mathsf{supp}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\subseteq\Sigma
,
\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~u\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)=(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\cdot u
by
B.1
. So,

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~u,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\big\{(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\cdot u+m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\mid m\in\varphi\big\}

\displaystyle=\exists m:\varphi.\>\mathbf{1}((\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\odot u)\>\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))

We know
\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\vDash u=u
, so the full derivation is given by:

\small\frac{\forall m\in\varphi.\>\>\frac{\frac{\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\vDash u=u}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~u~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\odot u{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Assume}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~u~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right=Div, vdots=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~u~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>m)\odot u\>\oplus\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Choice, sep=0em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~u~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~u,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Exists}

•

C={C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}
. Note that
\varphi=\exists m.\>\varphi.\>\mathbf{1}(m)
. To use the
Iter
rule, we define the corresponding families of assertions, parametric on a weighting function
m
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{1}\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi\>C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big)

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{1}\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi\>C)^{n}\fatsemi\>\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big)

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\>\Uparrow\big(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\big)

\displaystyle\psi_{\infty}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbf{1}\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\infty}(m)

\displaystyle\triangleq\>\Uparrow\left(\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\right)

Observe that

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\big(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big)=\mathsf{post}((\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n},\mathbf{1}(m))

\displaystyle\psi_{\infty}(m)\oplus\zeta_{\infty}(m)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\left(\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)=\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle},\mathbf{1}(m))

Next, we show that the premises of
Iter
hold for these definitions:

–

(\psi_{n}(m))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}(m)
.

Consider a family
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}(m)
for all
n
. By definition of
\psi_{n}(m)
,
m_{n}
can only be one weighting function:
\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)

By
E.6
, this is precisely the stateful component of
\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
:

\displaystyle=\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\vDash\mathbf{1}\left(\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)=\psi_{\infty}(m)

–

(\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}(m)
.

Take a family
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m)
for all
n
. Then,
m_{n}\vDash\mathbf{1}\big(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\big)
, so
m_{n}=\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
. We have that

\displaystyle(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|m_{n}|\cdot\top)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

\displaystyle=\left(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right|\cdot\top\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})

By
E.6
, this is equal to
\textsf{div}\>?\>\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)
, which certainly satisfies
\Uparrow\left(\left\llbracket{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\right)=\zeta_{\infty}(m)
.

–

\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n+1}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

\displaystyle\varphi_{n+1}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n+1}(m)

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n+1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m))\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})\mid m^{\prime}\in\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m)\}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C,\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m))

By the induction hypothesis, this triple is derivable.

–

\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(m)
