\displaystyle=\{\textsf{term}\>?\>\left\llbracket(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n}\fatsemi\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m)\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(\textsf{term}\>?\>(\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C)^{n})\}

\displaystyle=\{\left\llbracket\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})\mid m^{\prime}\in\varphi_{n}(m)\}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{post}(\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime},\varphi_{n}(m))

By the induction hypothesis, this triple is derivable.

–

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it is clear that
\varphi_{n}(m)\vDash\textsf{term}
and
\zeta_{n}(m)\vDash\mathsf{div}
.

By definition,
\varphi_{0}(m)=\mathbf{1}\big(\textsf{term}\>?\>m\big)
and
\zeta_{0}(m)=\>\Uparrow(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))
, so

\varphi_{0}(m)\oplus\zeta_{0}(m)=\mathbf{1}(\textsf{term}\>?\>(m)+\textsf{div}\>?\>m)=\mathbf{1}(m)

Then,

\displaystyle\varphi=\exists m:\varphi.\>\varphi_{0}(m)\oplus\zeta_{0}(m)

\displaystyle\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle},\varphi)=\exists m:\varphi.\>\psi_{\infty}(m)\oplus\zeta_{\infty}(m)

The derivation
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
proceeds as follows:

\small\frac{\frac{\forall n.\>\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}(m)\oplus\zeta_{n+1}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~e^{\prime}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{vdots=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}(m)\oplus\zeta_{0}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}(m)\oplus\zeta_{\infty}(m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Iter,sep=-4em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~{C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}({C}^{\langle e,e^{\prime}\rangle},\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right=Exists}

•

C=a
. We assumed that
\Omega
contains all valid triples pertaining to atomic actions
a\in\textsf{Act}
, so
\Omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(a,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(a,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

∎

Theorem E.10 (Relative Completeness).

\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\implies\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

Proof.

We show that
\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)\implies\psi
. Suppose that
m\vDash\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)
. Then by definition, there must exist some
m^{\prime}
such that
m=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}\!\!(m^{\prime})
. Since
\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, we know that
m\vDash\psi
as well. Then, we can apply
Consequence
to derive the desired triple:

\frac{\frac{\Omega}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:spost-derivation}}\quad\qquad\qquad\mathsf{post}(C,\varphi)\implies\psi}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Appendix F
Rule Derivations

Lemma F.1.

The following rules are derivable

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Left=\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv} - Partial}\qquad\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv} - Total}

Proof.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P
is equivalent to:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P

\displaystyle=\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\mathbf{1}(m)=\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\mathbf{1}(\eta(\sigma))

For
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
,
\eta(\sigma)\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P
implies
\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Moreover,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q

\displaystyle=\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q

\displaystyle=\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q\>\>=\>\>\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q

The derivation is as follows:

\frac{\frac{\forall m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\forall\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m).\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=Scale}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Choice}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P.\>\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil.\>\oplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}m(\sigma)\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Exists}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\implies{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P
, so by a simple application of
Consequence
, we can derive the converse:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

So we see that the two triples
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
are really interchangeable in any derivation. Very similar derivations can be obtained to show that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
equivalent in the logic as well.

Lemma F.2.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:lisbon-triple-equiv}}

Proof.

For each
m\in{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P
, we can fix a state
\sigma_{m}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
such that
\sigma_{m}\in\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
as well. This must exist by definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P
. Let
u_{m}=m(\sigma)
be its assigned weight. Note that
u_{m}\neq\dsser{0}
. Then, we have as consequences:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P\iff

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>\mathbf{1}(m)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>(u_{m}\odot\mathbf{1}(\eta(\sigma_{m})))\oplus\top

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>(u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q)\oplus\top\iff

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q\oplus\top

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q

The derivation is as follows:

\frac{\frac{\forall m\in\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil.\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=Scale}\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=True}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(u_{m}\odot{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Choice, sep=4em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P.\>(u_{m}\odot\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\oplus\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Exists}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.3.

The following rules are derivable:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Seq-Lisbon}\qquad\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Seq-Total-Hoare}

Proof.

We show only the derivation for
Seq-Total-Hoare
– we derive
Seq-Lisbon
nearly identically using an application of
F.2
.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv} - Total}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}R{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}

∎

Lemma F.4.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{\varphi_{1}\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad\varphi_{2}\vDash\neg b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=If}

Proof.

First, we give a subderivation (1):

\frac{\frac{\frac{\varphi_{1}\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Assume}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 16.38895pt\frac{\varphi_{2}\vDash\neg b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Assume}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Choice}\quad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}

The proof of (2) is nearly identical. We complete the derivation with:

\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}\quad\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\neg b\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\oplus\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\oplus\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Plus}

∎

Lemma F.5.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land\neg b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=If-Hoare}\\

Proof.

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\vDash b
and
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)\vDash\neg b
. The derivation proceeds as follows:

\frac{\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\neg b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right={\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=\hyperref@@ii[rule:if]{{If}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.6.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land\neg b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=If-Lisbon}

Proof.

Subderivation (1):

\frac{\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b\hskip 8.19447pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.19447pt\dsser{0}\odot\top\vDash\neg b\hskip 7.97224pt\frac{\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 6.80565pt\textsc{Right=True}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.97224pt\textsc{Right=Scale}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=If}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\autoref{lemma:lisbon-triple-equiv}}

Part (2) is symmetrical.

\frac{\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right=\autoref{lemma:lisbon-triple-equiv}}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:lisbon-triple-equiv}, vdots=2em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(P\land b)\lor{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q\lor{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Disj, sep=-5em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}Q{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.7.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{\zeta\vDash\mathsf{div}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Div*}

Proof.

Since
\zeta\vDash\mathsf{div}
, we know that for all
m
such that
m\vDash\zeta
,
\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\}
. Then, these are equivalent:
\zeta=\exists m:\zeta.\>\Uparrow^{(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))}
. The derivation is as follows:

\frac{\frac{\forall m\in\zeta.\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow^{(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\Uparrow^{(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Div}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:\zeta.\>\Uparrow^{(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists m:\zeta.\>\Uparrow^{(m({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Exists}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.8.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}\quad(\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\varphi_{n}\vDash b\quad\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b\quad\zeta_{n}\vDash\mathsf{div}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}\oplus\psi_{0}\oplus\zeta_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}\oplus\zeta_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right={While}}

Proof.

We will use
Iter
to derive this rule. First, we give the following subderivation (
*
):

\frac{\frac{\varphi_{n}\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Left=Assume}\quad\frac{\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\cdot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Assume}\quad\frac{\zeta_{n}\vDash\mathsf{div}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Div*, vdots=3em}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Choice}

This gives us (1):

\frac{\frac{(*)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Seq}

And we derive (2) similarly:

\frac{\frac{\varphi_{n}\vDash b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\neg b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\cdot\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Assume}\quad\frac{\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\neg b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Assume}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\neg b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Choice}

Now, note that since
\varphi_{n}\vDash b
and
\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b
, it must be that
\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\vDash\mathsf{true}
. Recall also that
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
is sugar for
{C}^{\langle b,\neg b\rangle}
. The full derivation proceeds as follows:

\frac{\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\quad\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~b\fatsemi C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\frac{(2)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{assume}~\neg b~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}\oplus\psi_{0}\oplus\zeta_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{\infty}\oplus\zeta_{\infty}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=Iter}

∎

Lemma F.9.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right={Invariant}}

Proof.

We will derive this rule using
While
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)

\displaystyle\psi_{n}=\psi_{\infty}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}=\zeta_{\infty}

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists u.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}

We show that the necessary premises hold:

•

Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for all
n
. That is,
m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)
, so
\emptyset\subset\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})\subseteq(P\land\neg b)
. Then,

\emptyset\subset\mathsf{supp}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\right)=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{supp}(m_{n})\subseteq(P\land\neg b)

By definition,
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)=\psi_{\infty}
. Thus,
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}
.

•

Again, take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
for all
n
. Note that
\zeta_{\infty}=\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}=\{m\mid\mathsf{supp}(m)\subseteq\{{\mathord{\circlearrowright}}\}\}
. Thus, it always holds that

\left(\inf_{n\in N}|m_{n}|\cdot\top\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\vDash\zeta_{\infty}

We have
(\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}
.

•

Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\vDash b
,
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)\vDash\neg b
, and
\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}\vDash\mathsf{div}
for all
n
.

The derivation is as follows:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}(P\land b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv} - Partial}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Consequence}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=While}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.10.

The following rule is derivable:

\frac{\forall n<N.\quad\varphi_{n+1}\vDash b\quad\varphi_{0}\vDash\neg b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists N:\mathbb{N}.\>\varphi_{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Variant}

Proof.

Once again, we use
While
. For all
N
and
n
, define

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\varphi_{N-n}&\text{if }n<N\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\psi_{n}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\varphi_{0}&\text{if }n\in\{N,\infty\}\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}=\zeta_{\infty}

\displaystyle\triangleq 1_{0}

We give the necessary premises for the rule:

•

Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,
m_{N}\vDash\varphi_{0}
while
m_{n}\vDash\dsser{0}\odot\top
for
n\neq N
. So
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}=m_{N}\vDash\varphi_{0}
. This gives us
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}
.

•

Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
. We know
|m_{n}|=\dsser{0}
for all
n\geq N
, so

\left(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|m_{n}|\cdot\top\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=\dsser{0}\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\vDash\dsser{0}\odot\top

We have
(\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})\vDash\zeta_{\infty}
.

•

We have as premise that for all
n<N
,
\varphi_{n},(\dsser{0}\odot\top)\vDash b
, so
\varphi_{n}^{\prime}\vDash b
.
\varphi_{0},(\dsser{0}\odot\top)\vDash\neg b
, so
\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b
. And finally
\zeta_{n}=\dsser{0}\odot\top\vDash\mathsf{div}
.

To derive
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}^{\prime}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
for all
n
, we consider two cases: either
n<N
or
n\geq N
. We take these subderivations as part (1):

\frac{\frac{\forall m<N.\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{m+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\forall n<N.\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N-n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N-(n+1)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{\forall n<N.\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}^{\prime}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\par\frac{\frac{\frac{\>}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=True}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\cdot T{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\dsser{0}\odot\top{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Scale}}{\forall n\geq N.\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}^{\prime}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}

The derivation is completed as follows:

\frac{\forall N\in\mathbb{N}.\>\frac{\frac{(1)}{\forall n\in N.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}^{\prime}\oplus\psi_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=While}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists N:\mathbb{N}.\>\varphi_{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{0}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Exists}

∎

Lemma F.11.

The following rule is derivable:

\hskip-48.36958pt\frac{(P\land b)\Rightarrow R>0\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\>\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\land R=n\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R<n){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land R\leq N\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Hoare-Variant}

Proof.

We use
While
and fix families of assertions
\varphi_{n}
,
\psi_{n}
and
\zeta_{n}
for the rule.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\zeta_{n}\triangleq\dsser{0}\odot\top
and

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}\triangleq\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq(N-n)\land b)&\text{if }n\leq N\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\text{if }n>N\end{cases}

\displaystyle\psi_{n}\triangleq\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq(N-n)\land\neg b)&\text{if }n\leq N\\
\dsser{0}\odot\top&\text{if }n>N\end{cases}

Let
\psi_{\infty}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)
and
\zeta_{\infty}\triangleq\dsser{0}\odot\top
.

We clearly have that
\varphi_{n}\vDash b
and
\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b
. Next, we show that the necessary convergence/divergence conditions hold:

•

Take any
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for all
n
. Then
m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)
, so
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b)
. This gives us
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}
.

•

Take
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{n}\vDash\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
for all
n
. Then for
m>N
,
m_{n}\vDash\dsser{0}\cdot\top
. So,
(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|m_{n}|\cdot\top\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})=0\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}}))
. Thus,
(\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}
.

Observe that for
n>N
,
\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}=\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land R\leq(N-n)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. In part (1) below, we derive the triple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{n+1}\oplus\psi_{n+1}\oplus\zeta_{n+1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
:

\frac{\forall m\leq N-n.\quad\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\land R=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R<m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b\land R=m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R<m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:hoare-triple-equiv} - Total}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b\land R=m){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq(N-(n+1))){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b\land R\leq(N-n)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq(N-(n+1))){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=Exists}

We then complete the derivation with an application of
While
and
consequence
:

\frac{\frac{\frac{(1)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land b\land R\leq(N-n)){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq(N-(n+1))){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land R\leq N){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=While}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land R\leq N\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(P\land\neg b){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=Consequence}

∎

Lemma F.12.

The following rules are derivable:

\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right={QInv-Demon}}\par\frac{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right={QInv-Angel}}

Proof.

We show the full derivation for
QInv-Angel
, omitting that for the demonic counterpart.

We use the
While
rule. For all
n
, take

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}

\displaystyle\triangleq(\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}^{(u)})\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}b

\displaystyle\psi_{n}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(\neg b)

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}

\displaystyle=\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}

\displaystyle\psi_{\infty}

\displaystyle\triangleq\top

\displaystyle\zeta_{\infty}

\displaystyle\triangleq\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}

Take any family of weighting functions
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

•

If
m_{n}\vDash\psi_{n}
for all
n
, it is trivial that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}m_{n}\vDash\top
. So
(\varphi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\rightsquigarrow\psi_{\infty}
.

•

Suppose
m_{n}\vDash\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
. Then,
m_{n}=p+q
where
p\vDash\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}^{(u)}=\varphi_{n}
, so
|m_{n}|>|p|>0
. This means that, for weight
v>0
,

\left(\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|m_{n}\cdot\top|\right)\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\>\>=\>\>v\cdot\eta({\mathord{\circlearrowright}})\>\>\vDash\>\>\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\Uparrow^{(u)}

Thus,
(\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Uparrow\zeta_{\infty}
.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vDash b
, we know
\varphi_{n}=\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}^{(u)}\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}b\vDash b
. Clearly,
\psi_{n}\vDash\neg b
and
\zeta_{n}\vDash\mathsf{div}
.

Note that we can partition
\top={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}b\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(\neg b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}
. So, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}\oplus\psi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}

\displaystyle=(\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}^{(u)}\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}b)\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(\neg b)\oplus\exists u:U.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}

\displaystyle=\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}^{(u)}\oplus\top

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P

\displaystyle\psi_{\infty}\oplus\zeta_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\exists u:U\setminus\{\dsser{0}\}.\>\Uparrow^{(u)}\oplus\top\>\>=\>\>{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}\mathsf{false}

Moreover,
{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P\land{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}b=\varphi_{n}\oplus\zeta_{n}
. The derivation is as follows:

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right=\autoref{lemma:lisbon-triple-equiv}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P\land{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}b{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=Consequence}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}P{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{Right=While}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=Consequence}

∎

Appendix G
Additional Case Studies

G.1.
Proving Nontermination

\displaystyle\textsc{Nt}\triangleq\begin{cases}&x:=1\fatsemi y:=2\fatsemi\\
&\texttt{while $x+y>1$ do}\\
&\qquad x:=3-x\fatsemi\\
&\qquad y:=3-y\fatsemi\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad\textsc{MallocDiv}\triangleq\begin{cases}&\texttt{byte}*p\fatsemi\\
&p:=\>(\texttt{byte}*)\>\texttt{malloc}(\texttt{size}+16)\fatsemi\\
&\texttt{while $p$ = NULL do}\\
&\qquad p:=\>(\texttt{byte}*)\>\texttt{malloc}(\texttt{size}+16)\fatsemi\\
\end{cases}

Figure 9.
Two non-terminating programs.

(
Raad
et al., 2024
)
advocated the need for formal methods to prove nontermination, as many industrial codebases have bugs that cause programs to diverge and are difficult to discover with testing. Here, we use some of their examples to demonstrate how
TOL
can be used for nontermination proving.
Consider the
Nt
program in
Figure 9
. To show that this program indeed always diverges, we make use of the
QInv-Demon
rule to derive the triple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\textsc{Nt}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle x:=1\fatsemi y:=2\fatsemi

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x=1\land y=2\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\langle\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x+y=3\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad x:=3-x\fatsemi

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}3-x+y=3\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\textsc{Assign}

\displaystyle\qquad y:=3-y\fatsemi

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(3-x)+(3-y)=3\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\textsc{Assign}

\displaystyle\qquad\implies{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x+y=3\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}(x+y=3)\rangle}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\square}\mathsf{false}\rangle}

As a second example, we will use the rule
QInv-Angel
to establish the
possibility
of nontermination in the presence of branching. Consider a loop involving the
malloc
function in C as in the program
MallocDiv
in
Figure 9
.

MallocDiv
allocates memory in a loop, iterating until a successful call to
malloc
and
p
is set to a non-null value. Crucially,
malloc

may
fail each time, giving rise to a divergent execution. We can thus see that the assertion
p=\textsf{NULL}
is a (angelic) quasi-invariant for this loop; we have

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p=\texttt{NULL}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~p:=\>(\texttt{byte}*)\>\texttt{malloc}(\texttt{size}+16)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lozenge}(p=\textsf{NULL}){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p=\texttt{NULL}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~p=\texttt{NULL}~\texttt{do}~\ldots~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacklozenge}\mathsf{false}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\quad\textsc{right=\hyperref@@ii[rule:quasi-invariant]{{QInv-Angel}}}

And thus
MallocDiv
may diverge. Similar proofs of
guaranteed nontermination
can be derived using
QInv-Demon
.

G.2.
Full Proof of Quicksort Partition

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle i:=0\fatsemi j:=n\fatsemi

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i=0\land j=n\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\implies{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\langle\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i\geq 0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\rangle}

\displaystyle\texttt{while}~i\leq j~\texttt{do}~

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land i\leq j\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\langle\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\rangle}

if
A[i]\leq p
then

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land A[i]\leq p\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\alpha(i+1)\land\beta(j)\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad i:=i+1\fatsemi

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i=m-1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\>\>\hyperref@@ii[fig:inference-command]{\textsc{Assign}}

else if
A[j]\geq p
then

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land A[j]\geq p\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j-1)\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad j:=j-1\fatsemi

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i=m-1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\>\>\hyperref@@ii[fig:inference-command]{\textsc{Assign}}

else

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land A[i]>p\land A[j]<p\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\texttt{swap}(A,i,j)\fatsemi

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land A[j]>p\land A[i]<p\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\implies{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i+1)\land\beta(j-1)\land j-i=m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad i:=i+1\fatsemi j:=j-1

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i=m-2\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\>\>\hyperref@@ii[fig:inference-command]{\textsc{Assign}}

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i<m\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\qquad//\>\>\hyperref@@ii[rule:if]{\textsc{If}}

\displaystyle\qquad\implies{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land j-i<m)\rangle}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\blacksquare}(\alpha(i)\land\beta(j)\land i>j)\rangle}\qquad//\>\>\textsc{Hoare-Variant}

Figure 10.
Decorated proof of the
Partition
program.
